Now let me compile my findings into a comprehensive architectural report.

## Architectural Exploration Report: Go Tetris Project

### 1. Package Structure

**Directory Tree:**
```
/Users/gabadi/workspace/gamaleds/tetris/.worktrees/architect/
├── cmd/
│   ├── tetris/main.go                    # CLI entry point
│   └── acceptance-runner/main.go         # Acceptance test runner
├── pkg/
│   ├── game/                             # Pure game logic (testable)
│   │   ├── types.go                      # Enums: Phase, Rotation, Kind; value types
│   │   ├── session.go                    # Session (state machine + commands)
│   │   ├── board.go                      # Board (10×20 + 2-row buffer)
│   │   ├── piece.go                      # ActivePiece (movement, rotation, footprint)
│   │   ├── bag.go                        # Bag (7-bag randomizer)
│   │   ├── piece_data.go                 # SRS kick tables (data only)
│   │   └── *_test.go files               # Unit + property tests
│   │
│   └── tui/                              # Terminal UI (rendering + input)
│       ├── tui.go                        # Main game loop & orchestration
│       ├── terminal.go                   # Raw terminal mode (syscall)
│       ├── input.go                      # Key parsing from stdin
│       ├── dispatch.go                   # Command dispatch to Session
│       ├── render.go                     # Frame composition
│       ├── render_board.go               # Board cell rendering
│       ├── render_panels.go              # Hold/next piece preview panels
│       ├── render_overlays.go            # Game-over/paused overlays
│       ├── colors.go                     # ANSI color tokens
│       └── qa_*_test.go files            # Acceptance + QA tests
│
└── acceptance/                           # Gherkin-driven acceptance tests
    ├── game_lifecycle/steps_test.go
    ├── gravity_and_lock/steps_test.go
    ├── lines_and_score/steps_test.go
    ├── movement_and_rotation/steps_test.go
    └── soft_drop/steps_test.go
```

---

### 2. Import Dependency Graph

**pkg/game** (Pure logic layer):
- `session.go`: imports `math/rand`
- `bag.go`: imports `math/rand`
- `board.go`, `piece.go`, `types.go`: **no external imports** (only stdlib types)

**pkg/tui** (I/O boundary):
- `tui.go`: imports `fmt`, `math/rand`, `os`, `os/signal`, `sync`, `syscall`, `time`, **`pkg/game`**
- `terminal.go`: imports `os`, `syscall`, `unsafe` (raw terminal control)
- `input.go`: imports `os` (stdin)
- `dispatch.go`: imports **`pkg/game`**
- `render.go`, `render_board.go`, `render_panels.go`, `render_overlays.go`: all import `strings`, `fmt`, **`pkg/game`**
- `colors.go`: imports `fmt`

**cmd/tetris**:
- imports **`pkg/tui`** only

**Dependency direction:** 
```
cmd/tetris → pkg/tui → pkg/game
```

---

### 3. Interface Definitions

**pkg/game** exposes these public types/interfaces (implicit):

1. **Session** - state machine with command methods:
   - `NewSession(rng *rand.Rand) *Session`
   - Commands: `PlayerStartsGame()`, `PlayerMovesLeft()`, `PlayerRotatesCW()`, `PlayerHardDrops()`, `PlayerPauses()`, etc.
   - Queries: `CurrentPhase()`, `Snapshot() RenderState`

2. **RenderState** - pure-value snapshot for rendering (decouples pkg/tui from Session internals):
   ```go
   type RenderState struct {
       Phase Phase
       Board CellGrid
       Active *ActivePieceView
       FlashRows []int
       NextKind, HeldKind Kind
       HoldUsed bool
       Score, Level, LinesClearedTotal int
   }
   ```

3. **ActivePieceView** - read-only piece snapshot:
   ```go
   type ActivePieceView struct {
       Kind Kind
       Footprint []Position
       Ghost []Position
   }
   ```

4. **Types** (enums + value structs):
   - `Phase` (enum: Waiting, Playing, Paused, GameOver)
   - `Rotation` (enum: North, East, South, West)
   - `Kind` (enum: KindI..KindZ, with KindNone as zero)
   - `Position` struct `{Col, Row int}`
   - `CellGrid [20][10]Kind`

**pkg/tui** exposes:
- `Run() error` - game loop entry point
- Internal types: `Key` enum, `Frame`, `RawTerminal`

---

### 4. Dependency Direction & Coupling Analysis

**✓ CLEAN dependency flow:**

1. **pkg/game is pure logic** — no IO, no UI, no external dependencies beyond `math/rand`
   - Board, Piece, Session, Bag are all testable in isolation
   - Snapshot method creates pure-value interface for rendering

2. **pkg/tui imports pkg/game** — correct direction
   - `dispatch.go` calls Session commands
   - `render_*.go` read RenderState for display
   - Terminal I/O (input, terminal control) is isolated in `input.go` and `terminal.go`

3. **cmd/tetris imports only pkg/tui** — thin entry point
   - Responsibility: start the game loop, that's all

**No reverse coupling:** pkg/game never imports from pkg/tui ✓

---

### 5. Testable/Boundary Split

**Pure Logic (pkg/game)** — all testable:
- `types.go` — domain enums, position, grid snapshots
- `session.go` — state machine, all gameplay commands
- `board.go` — cell storage, line clear logic
- `piece.go` — collision, rotation (with SRS kicks from piece_data.go)
- `bag.go` — 7-bag randomization

**Test coverage:**
- Unit tests: `*_test.go` in pkg/game (game_mechanics_test.go, session_lifecycle_test.go, property_test.go)
- Acceptance tests: `acceptance/*/steps_test.go` (Gherkin-driven scenarios)
- QA tests: `pkg/tui/qa_*_test.go` (integration tests using RenderState)

**IO Boundary (pkg/tui)** — not in unit test scope:
- `terminal.go` — raw mode syscalls, TermSize queries
- `input.go` — stdin key parsing
- `tui.go` — game loop with signal handling, goroutines
- `render_*.go` — ANSI formatting (tested via snapshot tests, not behavioral)

**Key decoupling mechanism:**
`Session.Snapshot() RenderState` creates a value copy of game state that:
- Excludes internal fields (rng, lock timing internals)
- Captures board, piece, phase, score at one moment
- Enables deterministic renderer testing without full Session exposure

---

### 6. Architecture Constraints (from CLAUDE.md)

- **Single surface:** `GameBoard` is the only UI surface (no navigation)
- **Testable/boundary split:** Enforced ✓ (pure game logic separate from TUI)
- **Cell width:** Each board cell is 2 terminal columns (enforced in render_board.go)
- **Render priority:** active piece > ghost > locked cells > empty (in playingCell function)
- **No animation except line-clear flash:** Flash rows stored in RenderState for one tick
- **DAS timing:** Delegated to terminal autorepeat (167 ms initial, 33 ms interval in dispatch logic)

---

### 7. Key Files & Lines of Logic

| Module | Key Responsibility | File | Lines |
|--------|-------------------|------|-------|
| **Game Logic** | State machine, all rules | session.go | ~380 |
| | Board manipulation | board.go | ~130 |
| | Piece collision, rotation | piece.go | ~128 |
| | Randomization | bag.go | ~58 |
| **TUI** | Game loop orchestration | tui.go | ~108 |
| | Command dispatch | dispatch.go | ~63 |
| | Key input parsing | input.go | ~101 |
| | Frame rendering | render.go | ~80+ |
| | Board cell rendering | render_board.go | ~100 |
| | Panel rendering | render_panels.go | ~100+ |
| | Overlay rendering | render_overlays.go | ~100+ |

---

### 8. Coupling Concerns & Observations

**No critical violations found.** Architecture is well-structured:

1. ✓ **pkg/game is isolated** — testable, deterministic, no IO
2. ✓ **Dependency graph is acyclic** — cmd → tui → game
3. ✓ **Boundary is sharp** — RenderState decouples renderer from Session internals
4. ✓ **TUI doesn't leak details** — dispatch.go calls only Session methods, doesn't touch Board directly

**Minor observations:**

- `tui.go` is the orchestration hub — creates Session, manages event loop, calls handleKey/Tick/Render. This is appropriate for the main loop.
- All render functions take `game.RenderState` as primary input, ensuring they work from snapshots, not live state.
- Terminal control (raw mode, TermSize) is properly isolated in terminal.go.
- Session holds a `*rand.Rand` — intentional for deterministic testing with seeded RNG.

---

### 9. Test Architecture

**Separation of test types:**

1. **Unit tests** (`pkg/game/*_test.go`)
   - Test Session commands, Board mechanics, Piece collisions
   - Run concurrently with `-test.parallel`
   - Pure logic, deterministic

2. **Acceptance tests** (`acceptance/*/steps_test.go`)
   - Gherkin-driven scenarios (feature files loaded as JSON)
   - Full game lifecycle scenarios
   - Run **sequentially** (never concurrent with unit tests per CLAUDE.md)

3. **QA tests** (`pkg/tui/qa_*_test.go`)
   - Integration tests combining game logic + rendering
   - Test that RenderState produces expected Frame output
   - Per-feature test files (qa_soft_drop_test.go, qa_gravity_lock_test.go, etc.)

---

### 10. Summary

This is a **well-architected Go project** with clear separation of concerns:

- **Pure logic (pkg/game):** Completely testable, zero external dependencies beyond stdlib rand
- **UI boundary (pkg/tui):** All IO confined; uses RenderState as immutable interface to game state
- **Dependency flow:** Clean (cmd → tui → game), no cycles, no reverse coupling
- **Single surface principle:** Enforced; only GameBoard UI surface exists

The architecture follows the constraints documented in CLAUDE.md and is properly factored for mutation testing (with mutate4go manifests already in place).

package tui

import (
	"fmt"
	"math/rand"
	"os"
	"os/signal"
	"sync"
	"syscall"
	"time"

	"github.com/gamaleds/tetris/pkg/game"
)

const (
	minCols = 40
	minRows = 24
	tickHz  = 10 // gravity ticks per second (simplified; full speed curve deferred)

	// Spec DAS timing (EXPERIENCE.md). Raw-mode terminal reads carry no
	// key-release events, so an internal repeat timer cannot know when the
	// key is let go — it runs away and glides the piece into the wall.
	// Hold-repeat is therefore delegated to the terminal's own key
	// autorepeat (one move per key event) until release detection exists
	// (e.g. kitty keyboard protocol).
	dasDelay    = 167 * time.Millisecond
	dasInterval = 33 * time.Millisecond
)

// Run starts the game loop. It blocks until the user exits (Ctrl+C).
func Run() error {
	rng := rand.New(rand.NewSource(time.Now().UnixNano()))
	s := game.NewSession(rng)

	rt, err := EnableRaw()
	if err != nil {
		return fmt.Errorf("raw terminal: %w", err)
	}
	defer rt.Restore()

	// Hide cursor, clear screen.
	fmt.Fprint(os.Stdout, "\x1b[?25l\x1b[2J")
	defer fmt.Fprint(os.Stdout, "\x1b[?25h\x1b[2J") // show cursor, clear on exit

	// Signal handler for clean exit.
	sigCh := make(chan os.Signal, 1)
	signal.Notify(sigCh, syscall.SIGTERM, syscall.SIGINT)

	// Key channel.
	keyCh := make(chan Key, 16)
	go func() {
		for {
			k := ReadKey()
			keyCh <- k
		}
	}()

	ticker := time.NewTicker(time.Second / tickHz)
	defer ticker.Stop()

	var mu sync.Mutex
	lastW, lastH := TermSize()
	tooSmall := lastW < minCols || lastH < minRows

	redraw := func() {
		w, h := TermSize()
		lastW, lastH = w, h
		tooSmall = w < minCols || h < minRows
		var f Frame
		if tooSmall {
			f = RenderTooSmall(w, h)
		} else {
			f = Render(s.Snapshot(), w, h)
		}
		fmt.Fprint(os.Stdout, f.String())
	}

	redraw()

	for {
		select {
		case <-sigCh:
			return nil

		case k := <-keyCh:
			if k == KeyCtrlC {
				return nil
			}
			mu.Lock()
			handleKey(s, k, tooSmall)
			redraw()
			mu.Unlock()

		case <-ticker.C:
			mu.Lock()
			w, h := TermSize()
			if w != lastW || h != lastH {
				handleResize(s, w, h)
			}
			if s.CurrentPhase() == game.PhasePlaying {
				s.Tick()
			}
			redraw()
			mu.Unlock()
		}
	}
}
```

package tui

import "github.com/gamaleds/tetris/pkg/game"

// handleResize pauses the game if the terminal shrinks below the minimum dimensions
// while the game is playing.
func handleResize(s *game.Session, w, h int) {
	if w < minCols || h < minRows {
		if s.CurrentPhase() == game.PhasePlaying {
			s.PlayerPauses()
		}
	}
}

// handleKey dispatches a key event to the session.
func handleKey(s *game.Session, k Key, tooSmall bool) {
	if tooSmall {
		return
	}
	switch s.CurrentPhase() {
	case game.PhaseWaiting:
		if k == KeyEnter {
			s.PlayerStartsGame()
		}
	case game.PhasePlaying:
		handlePlayingKey(s, k)
	case game.PhasePaused:
		if k == KeyP || k == KeyEsc {
			s.PlayerResumes()
		}
	case game.PhaseGameOver:
		if k == KeyR {
			s.PlayerRestartsGame()
		}
	}
}

// handlePlayingKey dispatches a key event while the game is in the playing phase.
func handlePlayingKey(s *game.Session, k Key) {
	switch k {
	case KeyLeft:
		s.PlayerMovesLeft()
	case KeyRight:
		s.PlayerMovesRight()
	case KeyUp, KeyX:
		s.PlayerRotatesCW()
	case KeyZ:
		s.PlayerRotatesCCW()
	case KeyDown:
		s.PlayerSoftDrops()
	case KeySpace:
		s.PlayerHardDrops()
	case KeyC:
		s.PlayerHolds()
	case KeyP, KeyEsc:
		s.PlayerPauses()
	}
}

package game

import "math/rand"

// Session holds all mutable game state. It is the single source of truth that
// maps to tetris.allium's GameSession entity.
type Session struct {
	Phase              Phase
	Board              *Board
	ActivePiece        *ActivePiece // nil when phase != playing|paused
	Bag                *Bag         // nil when phase != playing|paused
	NextKind           Kind         // KindNone on first spawn; set to bag.Peek() after first draw
	HeldKind           Kind         // KindNone = nothing held
	HoldUsed           bool
	Score              int
	Level              int
	LinesClearedTotal  int
	LockTicksRemaining int
	FlashRows          []int // non-nil during the one flash tick before row removal
	rng                *rand.Rand
}

// NewSession creates a session in the waiting phase with a seeded RNG.
func NewSession(rng *rand.Rand) *Session {
	return &Session{
		Phase:    PhaseWaiting,
		HeldKind: KindNone,
		NextKind: KindNone,
		rng:      rng,
	}
}

// --- Commands (actions that mutate state) ---

// PlayerStartsGame implements the GameStarts rule.
// Returns false and leaves state unchanged if phase != waiting.
func (s *Session) PlayerStartsGame() bool {
	if s.Phase != PhaseWaiting {
		return false
	}
	s.Phase = PhasePlaying
	s.Score = 0
	s.Level = 1
	s.LinesClearedTotal = 0
	s.HoldUsed = false
	s.HeldKind = KindNone
	s.Board = NewBoard()
	s.Bag = NewBag(s.rng)
	s.NextKind = KindNone

	s.bagRefilled()
	s.pieceSpawned()
	return true
}

// PlayerRestartsGame implements the GameRestarted rule.
// Returns false if phase != game_over.
func (s *Session) PlayerRestartsGame() bool {
	if s.Phase != PhaseGameOver {
		return false
	}
	s.Phase = PhaseWaiting
	s.PlayerStartsGame()
	return true
}

// SpawnPiece triggers PieceSpawned() — called by the game loop after a piece locks.
func (s *Session) SpawnPiece() {
	s.pieceSpawned()
}

// Tick advances the game by one frame.
func (s *Session) Tick() {
	if s.Phase != PhasePlaying {
		return
	}
	// Commit tick: FlashRows pending means rows are ready to be cleared and a new
	// piece must spawn. This guard must precede the ActivePiece == nil return
	// because ActivePiece is nil during the flash tick.
	if len(s.FlashRows) > 0 {
		s.Board.ClearRows(s.FlashRows)
		s.FlashRows = nil
		s.pieceSpawned()
		return
	}
	if s.ActivePiece == nil {
		return
	}
	if s.ActivePiece.AtRest(s.Board) {
		if s.LockTicksRemaining > 1 {
			s.LockTicksRemaining--
		} else {
			s.lockPiece()
		}
	} else {
		s.ActivePiece.Origin.Row++
	}
}

// PlayerMovesLeft implements PlayerMovesLeft rule.
func (s *Session) PlayerMovesLeft() bool {
	if s.Phase != PhasePlaying || s.ActivePiece == nil {
		return false
	}
	if !s.ActivePiece.CanMoveLeft(s.Board) {
		return false
	}
	s.ActivePiece.Origin.Col--
	if s.ActivePiece.AtRest(s.Board) {
		s.LockTicksRemaining = LockTicks
	}
	return true
}

// PlayerMovesRight implements PlayerMovesRight rule.
func (s *Session) PlayerMovesRight() bool {
	if s.Phase != PhasePlaying || s.ActivePiece == nil {
		return false
	}
	if !s.ActivePiece.CanMoveRight(s.Board) {
		return false
	}
	s.ActivePiece.Origin.Col++
	if s.ActivePiece.AtRest(s.Board) {
		s.LockTicksRemaining = LockTicks
	}
	return true
}

// PlayerRotatesCW implements PlayerRotatesCW rule.
func (s *Session) PlayerRotatesCW() bool {
	if s.Phase != PhasePlaying || s.ActivePiece == nil {
		return false
	}
	newOrigin, newRot, ok := s.ActivePiece.TryRotateCW(s.Board)
	if !ok {
		return false
	}
	s.ActivePiece.Origin = newOrigin
	s.ActivePiece.Rotation = newRot
	if s.ActivePiece.AtRest(s.Board) {
		s.LockTicksRemaining = LockTicks
	}
	return true
}

// PlayerRotatesCCW implements PlayerRotatesCCW rule.
func (s *Session) PlayerRotatesCCW() bool {
	if s.Phase != PhasePlaying || s.ActivePiece == nil {
		return false
	}
	newOrigin, newRot, ok := s.ActivePiece.TryRotateCCW(s.Board)
	if !ok {
		return false
	}
	s.ActivePiece.Origin = newOrigin
	s.ActivePiece.Rotation = newRot
	if s.ActivePiece.AtRest(s.Board) {
		s.LockTicksRemaining = LockTicks
	}
	return true
}

// PlayerSoftDrops implements PlayerSoftDrops rule.
func (s *Session) PlayerSoftDrops() bool {
	if s.Phase != PhasePlaying || s.ActivePiece == nil {
		return false
	}
	if s.ActivePiece.AtRest(s.Board) {
		return false
	}
	s.ActivePiece.Origin.Row++
	s.Score += SoftDropScore
	return true
}

// PlayerHardDrops implements HardDropLocks rule.
func (s *Session) PlayerHardDrops() bool {
	if s.Phase != PhasePlaying || s.ActivePiece == nil {
		return false
	}
	ghost := s.ActivePiece.GhostOrigin(s.Board)
	rowsDropped := ghost.Row - s.ActivePiece.Origin.Row
	s.ActivePiece.Origin = ghost
	s.Score += rowsDropped * HardDropScore
	s.lockPiece()
	return true
}

// PlayerPauses implements PlayerPauses rule.
func (s *Session) PlayerPauses() bool {
	if s.Phase != PhasePlaying {
		return false
	}
	s.Phase = PhasePaused
	return true
}

// PlayerResumes implements PlayerResumes rule.
func (s *Session) PlayerResumes() bool {
	if s.Phase != PhasePaused {
		return false
	}
	s.Phase = PhasePlaying
	return true
}

// PlayerHolds implements PlayerHoldsNoHeld and PlayerHoldsSwap rules.
func (s *Session) PlayerHolds() bool {
	if s.Phase != PhasePlaying || s.HoldUsed || s.ActivePiece == nil {
		return false
	}
	currentKind := s.ActivePiece.Kind
	s.ActivePiece = nil

	if s.HeldKind == KindNone {
		s.HeldKind = currentKind
		s.pieceSpawned()
		s.HoldUsed = true // override reset from pieceSpawned
	} else {
		returning := s.HeldKind
		s.HeldKind = currentKind
		s.ActivePiece = &ActivePiece{
			Kind:     returning,
			Origin:   Position{Col: SpawnCol, Row: SpawnRow},
			Rotation: RotationNorth,
		}
		s.HoldUsed = true
	}
	return true
}

// CurrentPhase returns the current session phase without exposing internal field layout
// to callers outside pkg/game.
func (s *Session) CurrentPhase() Phase {
	return s.Phase
}

// Snapshot returns a pure-value RenderState for the renderer.
func (s *Session) Snapshot() RenderState {
	rs := RenderState{
		Phase:             s.Phase,
		NextKind:          s.NextKind,
		HeldKind:          s.HeldKind,
		HoldUsed:          s.HoldUsed,
		Score:             s.Score,
		Level:             s.Level,
		LinesClearedTotal: s.LinesClearedTotal,
	}
	if len(s.FlashRows) > 0 {
		rs.FlashRows = append([]int(nil), s.FlashRows...)
	}
	if s.Board != nil {
		snapshotBoard(s.Board, &rs.Board)
	}
	if s.ActivePiece != nil && s.Phase == PhasePlaying {
		rs.Active = snapshotActivePiece(s.ActivePiece, s.Board)
	}
	return rs
}

func snapshotBoard(b *Board, grid *CellGrid) {
	for r := 0; r < BoardRows; r++ {
		for c := 0; c < BoardCols; c++ {
			grid[r][c] = b.Cell(c, r)
		}
	}
}

func snapshotActivePiece(ap *ActivePiece, b *Board) *ActivePieceView {
	fp := ap.Footprint()
	fpSet := make(map[Position]bool, len(fp))
	for _, p := range fp {
		fpSet[p] = true
	}
	ghostOrigin := ap.GhostOrigin(b)
	ghostPiece := &ActivePiece{Kind: ap.Kind, Origin: ghostOrigin, Rotation: ap.Rotation}
	ghost := ghostFPExcluding(ghostPiece.Footprint(), fpSet)
	return &ActivePieceView{Kind: ap.Kind, Footprint: fp, Ghost: ghost}
}

func ghostFPExcluding(ghostFP []Position, exclude map[Position]bool) []Position {
	var ghost []Position
	for _, p := range ghostFP {
		if !exclude[p] {
			ghost = append(ghost, p)
		}
	}
	return ghost
}

// --- Internal helpers ---

// bagRefilled implements BagRefilled() internal event.
func (s *Session) bagRefilled() {
	s.Bag.Refill()
}

// pieceSpawned implements PieceSpawned() internal event per the allium rule.
func (s *Session) pieceSpawned() {
	var kind Kind
	if s.NextKind == KindNone {
		// First spawn: draw directly from bag.
		if s.Bag.Empty() {
			s.bagRefilled()
		}
		kind = s.Bag.Draw()
	} else {
		kind = s.NextKind
		if !s.Bag.Empty() {
			s.Bag.Draw() // consume the peeked element
		}
	}

	// Advance bag for next preview; refill if needed.
	if s.Bag.Empty() {
		s.bagRefilled()
	}
	s.NextKind = s.Bag.Peek()

	s.ActivePiece = &ActivePiece{
		Kind:     kind,
		Origin:   Position{Col: SpawnCol, Row: SpawnRow},
		Rotation: RotationNorth,
	}
	s.HoldUsed = false
	s.LockTicksRemaining = LockTicks

	if !s.ActivePiece.ValidPlacement(s.Board) {
		s.gameOver()
	}
}

// gameOver implements GameOver() internal event.
func (s *Session) gameOver() {
	s.Phase = PhaseGameOver
}

// lockPiece implements PieceLockedOnBoard() internal event.
func (s *Session) lockPiece() {
	if s.ActivePiece == nil {
		return
	}
	for _, p := range s.ActivePiece.Footprint() {
		s.Board.SetCell(p.Col, p.Row, s.ActivePiece.Kind)
	}
	s.ActivePiece = nil
	s.linesCleared()
}

// linesCleared implements LinesCleared() internal event.
// When rows are cleared: stats update immediately; rows stay on the board for one
// flash tick (stored in FlashRows); ClearRows and spawn defer to the next Tick.
// When no rows are cleared: spawn happens synchronously.
func (s *Session) linesCleared() {
	full := s.Board.FullRows()
	if len(full) > 0 {
		s.scoreAwarded(len(full))
		s.LinesClearedTotal += len(full)
		s.levelProgressed()
		s.FlashRows = full // commit tick will call ClearRows + pieceSpawned
		return
	}
	s.pieceSpawned()
}

// scoreAwarded implements ScoreAwarded(count) rule.
func (s *Session) scoreAwarded(count int) {
	multiplier := map[int]int{1: 100, 2: 300, 3: 500, 4: 800}[count]
	s.Score += multiplier * s.Level
}

// levelProgressed implements LevelProgressed() rule.
func (s *Session) levelProgressed() {
	earned := (s.LinesClearedTotal/LinesPerLevel) + 1
	if earned > s.Level {
		s.Level = earned
	}
}

package game

// Phase represents the current game session state.
type Phase int

const (
	PhaseWaiting  Phase = iota
	PhasePlaying
	PhasePaused
	PhaseGameOver
)

var phaseNames = [...]string{"waiting", "playing", "paused", "game_over"}

func (p Phase) String() string {
	if int(p) < len(phaseNames) {
		return phaseNames[p]
	}
	return "unknown"
}

// Rotation of a piece.
type Rotation int

const (
	RotationNorth Rotation = iota
	RotationEast
	RotationSouth
	RotationWest
)

var rotationNames = [...]string{"north", "east", "south", "west"}

func (r Rotation) String() string {
	if int(r) < len(rotationNames) {
		return rotationNames[r]
	}
	return "unknown"
}

// Kind identifies a tetromino piece type. KindNone must be zero so that
// a zero-initialized board array represents an empty board.
type Kind int

const (
	KindNone Kind = iota // sentinel — held_kind = none, or empty cell
	KindI
	KindJ
	KindL
	KindO
	KindS
	KindT
	KindZ
)

var AllKinds = [7]Kind{KindI, KindJ, KindL, KindO, KindS, KindT, KindZ}

var kindNames = [...]string{"none", "i", "j", "l", "o", "s", "t", "z"}

func (k Kind) String() string {
	if int(k) < len(kindNames) {
		return kindNames[k]
	}
	return "unknown"
}

// Position is a (col, row) coordinate. Row may be negative (spawn buffer).
type Position struct {
	Col int
	Row int
}

// Config holds compile-time constants matching tetris.allium config block.
const (
	BoardCols     = 10
	BoardRows     = 20
	SpawnCol      = 3
	SpawnRow      = -2
	LinesPerLevel = 10
	LockTicks     = 30
	SoftDropScore = 1
	HardDropScore = 2
)

// CellGrid is a 10×20 snapshot of the locked board state for rendering.
type CellGrid [BoardRows][BoardCols]Kind

// ActivePieceView is a read-only snapshot of the active piece for rendering.
type ActivePieceView struct {
	Kind      Kind
	Footprint []Position // absolute board positions
	Ghost     []Position // ghost positions (excludes active footprint)
}

// RenderState is a pure value snapshot of session state for the renderer.
// It is owned by pkg/game and decouples pkg/tui from *Session internals.
type RenderState struct {
	Phase             Phase
	Board             CellGrid
	Active            *ActivePieceView // nil when not playing
	FlashRows         []int            // row indices flashing white this tick; nil when no flash
	NextKind          Kind
	HeldKind          Kind
	HoldUsed          bool
	Score             int
	Level             int
	LinesClearedTotal int
}

package game

// SpawnBufferRows is the number of hidden rows above the visible board.
// Pieces spawn at row SpawnRow = -2, so the buffer must cover rows -2..-1.
const SpawnBufferRows = 2

// totalRows is the full internal storage height: buffer + visible.
const totalRows = SpawnBufferRows + BoardRows

// Board is the 10×20 visible play field plus a 2-row hidden spawn buffer.
// Internal storage uses rows 0..totalRows-1; public API uses row indices
// where 0 is the topmost visible row and negative indices address the buffer.
type Board struct {
	// cells[internalRow][col]; internalRow = row + SpawnBufferRows
	cells [totalRows][BoardCols]Kind
}

// NewBoard returns a blank board.
func NewBoard() *Board {
	return &Board{}
}

func (b *Board) toInternal(row int) int {
	return row + SpawnBufferRows
}

// Cell returns the Kind at (col, row). Returns KindNone for out-of-range rows.
func (b *Board) Cell(col, row int) Kind {
	ir := b.toInternal(row)
	if ir < 0 || ir >= totalRows {
		return KindNone
	}
	return b.cells[ir][col]
}

// SetCell places a kind at (col, row). No-op for out-of-range rows.
func (b *Board) SetCell(col, row int, k Kind) {
	ir := b.toInternal(row)
	if ir < 0 || ir >= totalRows {
		return
	}
	b.cells[ir][col] = k
}

// CellOccupied reports whether (col, row) contains a locked cell.
// Returns true for column out-of-bounds or row below the bottom.
// Returns false for rows above the buffer top (shouldn't occur in play).
func (b *Board) CellOccupied(col, row int) bool {
	if col < 0 || col >= BoardCols {
		return true
	}
	ir := b.toInternal(row)
	if ir < 0 {
		return false
	}
	if ir >= totalRows {
		return true
	}
	return b.cells[ir][col] != KindNone
}

// FullRows returns visible row indices (0-indexed) where every cell is filled.
func (b *Board) FullRows() []int {
	var full []int
	for r := 0; r < BoardRows; r++ {
		if b.isRowFull(r) {
			full = append(full, r)
		}
	}
	return full
}

func (b *Board) isRowFull(row int) bool {
	ir := b.toInternal(row)
	for c := 0; c < BoardCols; c++ {
		if b.cells[ir][c] == KindNone {
			return false
		}
	}
	return true
}

// ClearRows removes the given visible rows and drops everything above them.
// Rows must be provided in ascending order.
func (b *Board) ClearRows(rows []int) {
	if len(rows) == 0 {
		return
	}
	cleared := make(map[int]bool, len(rows))
	for _, r := range rows {
		cleared[r] = true
	}

	// Rebuild internal storage from bottom up, skipping cleared rows.
	var next [totalRows][BoardCols]Kind
	dst := totalRows - 1
	for src := totalRows - 1; src >= 0; src-- {
		visibleRow := src - SpawnBufferRows
		if cleared[visibleRow] {
			continue
		}
		next[dst] = b.cells[src]
		dst--
	}
	b.cells = next
}

// FilledCells returns all visible (col, row, kind) tuples where kind != KindNone.
func (b *Board) FilledCells() []struct {
	Col, Row int
	Kind     Kind
} {
	var out []struct {
		Col, Row int
		Kind     Kind
	}
	for r := 0; r < BoardRows; r++ {
		ir := b.toInternal(r)
		for c := 0; c < BoardCols; c++ {
			if b.cells[ir][c] != KindNone {
				out = append(out, struct {
					Col, Row int
					Kind     Kind
				}{c, r, b.cells[ir][c]})
			}
		}
	}
	return out
}

package game

// Footprint returns the absolute board positions for kind at origin+rotation.
// k must be KindI..KindZ (not KindNone).
func Footprint(k Kind, origin Position, rot Rotation) []Position {
	offsets := pieceFootprints[k-1][rot]
	out := make([]Position, len(offsets))
	for i, o := range offsets {
		out[i] = Position{Col: origin.Col + o.Col, Row: origin.Row + o.Row}
	}
	return out
}

// ActivePiece is the falling piece currently controlled by the player.
type ActivePiece struct {
	Kind     Kind
	Origin   Position
	Rotation Rotation
}

// Footprint returns the absolute board positions of this piece.
func (ap *ActivePiece) Footprint() []Position {
	return Footprint(ap.Kind, ap.Origin, ap.Rotation)
}

// ValidPlacement reports whether all footprint cells are in-column-bounds and
// not occupied on the board, including the hidden spawn buffer rows.
func (ap *ActivePiece) ValidPlacement(b *Board) bool {
	for _, p := range ap.Footprint() {
		if b.CellOccupied(p.Col, p.Row) {
			return false
		}
	}
	return true
}

// AtRest reports whether shifting origin.row by +1 would make ValidPlacement false.
func (ap *ActivePiece) AtRest(b *Board) bool {
	below := &ActivePiece{Kind: ap.Kind, Origin: Position{Col: ap.Origin.Col, Row: ap.Origin.Row + 1}, Rotation: ap.Rotation}
	return !below.ValidPlacement(b)
}

// GhostOrigin returns the lowest row at which the piece still has a valid placement.
func (ap *ActivePiece) GhostOrigin(b *Board) Position {
	ghost := &ActivePiece{Kind: ap.Kind, Origin: ap.Origin, Rotation: ap.Rotation}
	for {
		next := &ActivePiece{Kind: ap.Kind, Origin: Position{Col: ghost.Origin.Col, Row: ghost.Origin.Row + 1}, Rotation: ap.Rotation}
		if !next.ValidPlacement(b) {
			break
		}
		ghost.Origin = next.Origin
	}
	return ghost.Origin
}

// CanMoveLeft reports whether moving one column left is valid.
func (ap *ActivePiece) CanMoveLeft(b *Board) bool {
	moved := &ActivePiece{Kind: ap.Kind, Origin: Position{Col: ap.Origin.Col - 1, Row: ap.Origin.Row}, Rotation: ap.Rotation}
	return moved.ValidPlacement(b)
}

// CanMoveRight reports whether moving one column right is valid.
func (ap *ActivePiece) CanMoveRight(b *Board) bool {
	moved := &ActivePiece{Kind: ap.Kind, Origin: Position{Col: ap.Origin.Col + 1, Row: ap.Origin.Row}, Rotation: ap.Rotation}
	return moved.ValidPlacement(b)
}

// CWRotation returns the next clockwise rotation state.
func CWRotation(r Rotation) Rotation {
	return (r + 1) % 4
}

// CCWRotation returns the next counter-clockwise rotation state.
func CCWRotation(r Rotation) Rotation {
	return (r + 3) % 4
}

// kickTable returns the appropriate SRS kick table for this piece kind.
func kickTable(k Kind) *[4][4][5]Position {
	if k == KindI {
		return &srsKicksI
	}
	return &srsKicksJLSTZ
}

// TryRotateCW attempts a CW rotation using SRS kicks.
// Returns (newOrigin, newRotation, true) if a valid kick is found, or (_, _, false) if blocked.
func (ap *ActivePiece) TryRotateCW(b *Board) (Position, Rotation, bool) {
	return ap.tryRotate(b, CWRotation(ap.Rotation))
}

// TryRotateCCW attempts a CCW rotation using SRS kicks.
// Returns (newOrigin, newRotation, true) if a valid kick is found, or (_, _, false) if blocked.
func (ap *ActivePiece) TryRotateCCW(b *Board) (Position, Rotation, bool) {
	return ap.tryRotate(b, CCWRotation(ap.Rotation))
}

// CanRotateCW reports whether a CW rotation has at least one valid SRS kick.
func (ap *ActivePiece) CanRotateCW(b *Board) bool {
	_, _, ok := ap.TryRotateCW(b)
	return ok
}

// CanRotateCCW reports whether a CCW rotation has at least one valid SRS kick.
func (ap *ActivePiece) CanRotateCCW(b *Board) bool {
	_, _, ok := ap.TryRotateCCW(b)
	return ok
}

func (ap *ActivePiece) tryRotate(b *Board, toRot Rotation) (Position, Rotation, bool) {
	kicks := kickTable(ap.Kind)
	for _, offset := range kicks[ap.Rotation][toRot] {
		candidate := &ActivePiece{
			Kind:     ap.Kind,
			Origin:   Position{Col: ap.Origin.Col + offset.Col, Row: ap.Origin.Row + offset.Row},
			Rotation: toRot,
		}
		if candidate.ValidPlacement(b) {
			return candidate.Origin, toRot, true
		}
	}
	return Position{}, toRot, false
}

package game

import (
	"math/rand"
	"testing"
	"testing/quick"
)

// --- Rotation round-trip ---

func TestPropertyRotationRoundTrip(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(r uint8) bool {
		rot := Rotation(r % 4)
		return CCWRotation(CWRotation(rot)) == rot
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// --- Bag always contains all 7 kinds after refill ---

func TestPropertyBagRefillContainsAll7Kinds(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(seed int64) bool {
		b := NewBag(rand.New(rand.NewSource(seed)))
		b.Refill()
		seen := make(map[Kind]bool, 7)
		for !b.Empty() {
			seen[b.Draw()] = true
		}
		return len(seen) == 7
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// --- GhostOrigin row >= active piece origin row ---

func TestPropertyGhostRowAtLeastOriginRow(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(kindIdx uint8, col int8, rot uint8) bool {
		k := AllKinds[int(kindIdx)%7]
		r := Rotation(rot % 4)
		// Clamp col to valid range so the piece can have a valid placement.
		c := int(col)%BoardCols + BoardCols
		c = c % BoardCols
		ap := &ActivePiece{Kind: k, Origin: Position{Col: c, Row: SpawnRow}, Rotation: r}
		b := NewBoard()
		ghost := ap.GhostOrigin(b)
		return ghost.Row >= ap.Origin.Row
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

// --- Board: full rows always inside [0, BoardRows) ---

func TestPropertyFullRowsAlwaysInBounds(t *testing.T) {
	f := func(seed int64) bool {
		rng := rand.New(rand.NewSource(seed))
		b := NewBoard()
		// Randomly fill some cells.
		for r := 0; r < BoardRows; r++ {
			for c := 0; c < BoardCols; c++ {
				if rng.Intn(2) == 0 {
					b.SetCell(c, r, KindI)
				}
			}
		}
		for _, row := range b.FullRows() {
			if row < 0 || row >= BoardRows {
				return false
			}
		}
		return true
	}
	if err := quick.Check(f, nil); err != nil {
		t.Error(err)
	}
}

		}
		loadedFeature = &feat
	}

		return nil
	}

package tui

import (
	"strings"

	"github.com/gamaleds/tetris/pkg/game"
)

// Frame is a rendered terminal frame (one string per terminal row).
type Frame struct {
	rows []string
}

// Render computes a complete Frame for the given render state.
func Render(rs game.RenderState, termW, termH int) Frame {
	const (
		assemblyW = 40 // 8 + 1 + 22 + 1 + 8
		assemblyH = 22 // 20 cells + 2 border rows
	)

	lines := make([]string, assemblyH)
	buildAssembly(rs, lines)

	leftPad := 0
	if termW > assemblyW {
		leftPad = (termW - assemblyW) / 2
	}
	topPad := 0
	if termH > assemblyH {
		topPad = (termH - assemblyH) / 2
	}

	padStr := strings.Repeat(" ", leftPad)
	out := make([]string, termH)
	for i := range out {
		row := i - topPad
		if row >= 0 && row < assemblyH {
			out[i] = padStr + lines[row]
		} else {
			out[i] = padStr + strings.Repeat(" ", assemblyW)
		}
	}
	return Frame{rows: out}
}

// RenderTooSmall returns a frame for terminals smaller than 40×24.
func RenderTooSmall(termW, termH int) Frame {
	msg := "RESIZE TERMINAL"
	out := make([]string, termH)
	for i := range out {
		if i == termH/2 {
			pad := (termW - len(msg)) / 2
			if pad < 0 {
				pad = 0
			}
			out[i] = strings.Repeat(" ", pad) + colFgPrim + msg + reset
		} else {
			out[i] = ""
		}
	}
	return Frame{rows: out}
}

// String serializes the frame for terminal output.
func (f Frame) String() string {
	var sb strings.Builder
	sb.WriteString("\x1b[H")
	sb.WriteString(colBg)
	for i, row := range f.rows {
		if i > 0 {
			sb.WriteString("\r\n")
		}
		sb.WriteString(row)
		sb.WriteString(reset)
	}
	return sb.String()
}

// PlainRows returns frame rows stripped of ANSI escapes, for snapshot tests.
func (f Frame) PlainRows() []string {
	out := make([]string, len(f.rows))
	for i, r := range f.rows {
		out[i] = stripANSI(r)
	}
	return out
}

// stripANSI removes ANSI CSI escape sequences (ESC [ ... letter) from s.
func stripANSI(s string) string {
	var out strings.Builder
	for i := 0; i < len(s); {
		if isANSIEscapeStart(s, i) {
			i = skipANSIEscape(s, i+2)
		} else {
			out.WriteByte(s[i])
			i++
		}
	}
	return out.String()
}

func isANSIEscapeStart(s string, i int) bool {
	return s[i] == '\x1b' && i+1 < len(s) && s[i+1] == '['
}

func skipANSIEscape(s string, i int) int {
	for i < len(s) && !isANSITerminator(s[i]) {
		i++
	}
	if i < len(s) {
		i++
	}
	return i
}

func isANSITerminator(b byte) bool {
	return b >= 'A' && b <= 'z'
}

// --- Assembly builder ---

// Column layout: hold(8) + gap(1) + board(22) + gap(1) + right(8) = 40
func buildAssembly(rs game.RenderState, lines []string) {
	activeFP := map[game.Position]bool{}
	ghostFP := map[game.Position]bool{}
	var activePieceKind game.Kind
	if rs.Active != nil {
		activePieceKind = rs.Active.Kind
		for _, p := range rs.Active.Footprint {
			activeFP[p] = true
		}
		for _, p := range rs.Active.Ghost {
			ghostFP[p] = true
		}
	}

	const boardH = 22
	for row := 0; row < boardH; row++ {
		left := holdRow(rs, row)
		center := boardRow(rs, row, activeFP, ghostFP, activePieceKind)
		right := rightRow(rs, row)
		lines[row] = left + " " + center + " " + right
	}
}
```

package tui

import (
	"strings"

	"github.com/gamaleds/tetris/pkg/game"
)

// --- Board (22 cols wide, 22 rows tall) ---

func boardRow(rs game.RenderState, row int, activeFP, ghostFP map[game.Position]bool, activeKind game.Kind) string {
	if row == 0 {
		return colChrome + "┌" + strings.Repeat("─", 20) + "┐" + reset
	}
	if row == 21 {
		return colChrome + "└" + strings.Repeat("─", 20) + "┘" + reset
	}

	cellRow := row - 1 // board row 0..19
	var sb strings.Builder
	sb.WriteString(colChrome + "│" + reset)
	for col := 0; col < game.BoardCols; col++ {
		pos := game.Position{Col: col, Row: cellRow}
		sb.WriteString(cellStr(rs, pos, activeFP, ghostFP, activeKind))
	}
	sb.WriteString(colChrome + "│" + reset)
	return sb.String()
}

// cellStr returns the 2-char styled string for one board cell.
func cellStr(rs game.RenderState, pos game.Position, activeFP, ghostFP map[game.Position]bool, activeKind game.Kind) string {
	switch rs.Phase {
	case game.PhasePaused:
		return pausedCell(pos)
	case game.PhaseWaiting:
		return overlayCell(pos, waitingOverlayGrid())
	case game.PhaseGameOver:
		return gameOverCell(rs, pos)
	default:
		return playingCell(rs, pos, activeFP, ghostFP, activeKind)
	}
}

func pausedCell(pos game.Position) string {
	if pos.Row == 10 {
		pausedOverlay := pausedOverlayGrid()
		cell := pausedOverlay[pos.Row][pos.Col]
		if cell != "" {
			return cell
		}
	}
	return colFgDim + colBg + "░ " + reset
}

func gameOverCell(rs game.RenderState, pos game.Position) string {
	ov := gameOverOverlayGrid(rs.Score)
	if cell := overlayLookup(ov, pos); cell != "" {
		return cell
	}
	return frozenBoardCell(rs, pos)
}

func overlayLookup(g overlayGrid, pos game.Position) string {
	if pos.Row < 0 || pos.Row >= 20 || pos.Col < 0 || pos.Col >= 10 {
		return ""
	}
	return g[pos.Row][pos.Col]
}

func frozenBoardCell(rs game.RenderState, pos game.Position) string {
	k := rs.Board[pos.Row][pos.Col]
	if k != game.KindNone {
		return pieceColorLocked(int(k)) + "██" + reset
	}
	return colFgDim + colBg + "· " + reset
}

func playingCell(rs game.RenderState, pos game.Position, activeFP, ghostFP map[game.Position]bool, activeKind game.Kind) string {
	for _, flashRow := range rs.FlashRows {
		if pos.Row == flashRow {
			return colFlash + "██" + reset
		}
	}
	if activeFP[pos] {
		return pieceColorActive(int(activeKind)) + "██" + reset
	}
	if ghostFP[pos] {
		return pieceColorGhost(int(activeKind)) + "░░" + reset
	}
	k := rs.Board[pos.Row][pos.Col]
	if k != game.KindNone {
		return pieceColorLocked(int(k)) + "██" + reset
	}
	return colFgDim + colBg + "· " + reset
}

package game

import (
	"math/rand"
	"testing"
)

func startedSession() *Session {
	s := NewSession(rand.New(rand.NewSource(42)))
	s.PlayerStartsGame()
	return s
}

func TestPhaseString(t *testing.T) {
	cases := []struct {
		phase Phase
		want  string
	}{
		{PhaseWaiting, "waiting"},
		{PhasePlaying, "playing"},
		{PhasePaused, "paused"},
		{PhaseGameOver, "game_over"},
		{Phase(4), "unknown"},
		{Phase(99), "unknown"},
	}
	for _, tc := range cases {
		if got := tc.phase.String(); got != tc.want {
			t.Errorf("Phase(%d).String() = %q, want %q", tc.phase, got, tc.want)
		}
	}
}

func TestRotationString(t *testing.T) {
	cases := []struct {
		rot  Rotation
		want string
	}{
		{RotationNorth, "north"},
		{RotationEast, "east"},
		{RotationSouth, "south"},
		{RotationWest, "west"},
		{Rotation(4), "unknown"},
		{Rotation(99), "unknown"},
	}
	for _, tc := range cases {
		if got := tc.rot.String(); got != tc.want {
			t.Errorf("Rotation(%d).String() = %q, want %q", tc.rot, got, tc.want)
		}
	}
}

func TestKindString(t *testing.T) {
	cases := []struct {
		kind Kind
		want string
	}{
		{KindNone, "none"},
		{KindI, "i"},
		{KindJ, "j"},
		{KindL, "l"},
		{KindO, "o"},
		{KindS, "s"},
		{KindT, "t"},
		{KindZ, "z"},
		{Kind(8), "unknown"},
		{Kind(99), "unknown"},
	}
	for _, tc := range cases {
		if got := tc.kind.String(); got != tc.want {
			t.Errorf("Kind(%d).String() = %q, want %q", tc.kind, got, tc.want)
		}
	}
}

func TestBoardCell(t *testing.T) {
	b := NewBoard()
	b.SetCell(3, 5, KindI)
	if got := b.Cell(3, 5); got != KindI {
		t.Errorf("Cell(3,5) = %v, want I", got)
	}

// Acceptance tests for the game-lifecycle Gherkin feature.
// These tests correspond directly to game_lifecycle.feature scenarios.
// Run separately from unit tests: go test ./acceptance/...
//
// When GHERKIN_FEATURE_JSON is set, example values are loaded from that file,
// enabling gherkin-mutator to drive differential mutation.
package game_lifecycle_test

import (
	"encoding/json"
	"math/rand"
	"os"
	"strconv"
	"testing"

	"github.com/gamaleds/tetris/pkg/game"
)

// --- Feature JSON loader ---

type gherkinFeature struct {
	Scenarios []gherkinScenario `json:"scenarios"`
}

type gherkinScenario struct {
	Examples []map[string]string `json:"examples"`
}

var loadedFeature *gherkinFeature

func featureExamples(scenarioIndex, exampleIndex int) map[string]string {
	if loadedFeature == nil {
		path := os.Getenv("GHERKIN_FEATURE_JSON")
		if path == "" {
			return nil
		}
		f, err := os.Open(path)
		if err != nil {
			return nil
		}
		defer f.Close()
		var feat gherkinFeature
		if err := json.NewDecoder(f).Decode(&feat); err != nil {
			return nil
		}
		loadedFeature = &feat
	}
	if scenarioIndex >= len(loadedFeature.Scenarios) {
		return nil
	}

> AGENT

I see a significant architectural concern: the **acceptance tests directly mutate `Session` fields** (`s.ActivePiece = &game.ActivePiece{...}`, `s.Board.SetCell(...)`). This is a boundary violation — external packages are directly setting internal state that should be managed by Session's own state machine.

This is a real architectural gap. The acceptance tests need a way to set up specific game states for their scenarios, and currently they bypass the Session's public API to do it.

The architecture improvement here is to add **test setup helper methods** on `Session` that expose state injection for test scenarios, so external packages can use `Session.SetActiveForTest(kind, col, row, rot)` and `Session.SetBoardCellForTest(col, row, kind)` instead of direct field mutation. This would let the Session struct fields be made unexported.

However, this is a significant change. Let me assess the scope more carefully:

> AGENT

The architecture issue is clear: acceptance tests (in separate packages) directly mutate `Session` fields like `s.Score = 7`, `s.Level = level`, `s.ActivePiece = &game.ActivePiece{...}`, `s.Board.SetCell(...)`. This means Session's fields must remain public, which exposes internal mutable state.

The right architectural fix is to add a **seeding/injection interface** so tests can set up game state without direct field access. This could be done through test-only methods or a `SessionState` value that can be passed to a constructor.

Let me look at what specific state is being set to understand the right abstraction:

I now have a complete picture of the architectural situation. Let me analyze what's needed:

**Current state**: Session struct fields are public, required by:
1. Acceptance tests (external packages) that directly set `s.ActivePiece`, `s.Score`, `s.Level`, `s.Board.SetCell()`, `s.ActivePiece.Origin` etc.
2. The `game_lifecycle` test also sets `s.ActivePiece = nil` to trigger game-over

**The architectural improvement**: Add test-fixture methods to Session so external packages don't need direct field access. This way:
- `Session.SetActiveForTest(kind Kind, col, row int, rot Rotation)` 
- `Session.SetActivePosForTest(col, row int)` 
- `Session.SetBoardCellForTest(col, row int, k Kind)`
- `Session.SetScoreForTest(score int)`
- `Session.SetLevelForTest(level int, linesClearedTotal int)`
- `Session.ClearActivePieceForTest()` (used in game_lifecycle to trigger game over test setup)

Then gradually make the fields private. However, there are also read accesses that need query methods like `ActivePieceView()`, `ActivePiece().Origin`, etc.

Actually, looking more carefully — the `Snapshot()` method already provides `ActivePieceView` for reads. The issue is:
1. **Write access**: direct mutation of `s.ActivePiece`, `s.Score`, `s.Level`, `s.Board`
2. **Read access**: direct read of `s.ActivePiece.Origin`, `s.ActivePiece.Rotation`, `s.ActivePiece.Kind`, `s.Score`, `s.Level`, `s.LinesClearedTotal`, `s.Board.Cell()`

For the read accesses in test assertions, `Snapshot()` already provides all the needed info. For write access, test helper methods are needed.

This is a meaningful architectural improvement. Let me implement it carefully:

1. Add test fixture methods to `session.go`
2. Update acceptance tests to use those methods
3. Keep the field access in `game` package white-box tests (they're same package, that's fine)

But first — the `Session.Board` and `Session.ActivePiece` reads in acceptance tests could use `Snapshot()`. Let me check what they need:

The acceptance tests read:
- `s.ActivePiece.Origin.Row/Col` — assertion
- `s.ActivePiece.Rotation` — assertion  
- `s.ActivePiece.Kind` — assertion
- `s.Score` — assertion
- `s.Level` — assertion
- `s.LinesClearedTotal` — assertion
- `s.Board.Cell(col, row)` — assertion

All of these are already in `RenderState`/`Snapshot()`. The write access is the real boundary violation.

The right architectural approach: add `For-test` setter methods on `Session` and update acceptance tests to use `Snapshot()` for reads. This keeps `Session` fields private-able (they stay public for now but tests stop depending on that fact).

1	package game
2	
3	import "math/rand"
4	
5	// Session holds all mutable game state. It is the single source of truth that
6	// maps to tetris.allium's GameSession entity.
7	type Session struct {
8		Phase              Phase
9		Board              *Board
10		ActivePiece        *ActivePiece // nil when phase != playing|paused
11		Bag                *Bag         // nil when phase != playing|paused
12		NextKind           Kind         // KindNone on first spawn; set to bag.Peek() after first draw
13		HeldKind           Kind         // KindNone = nothing held
14		HoldUsed           bool
15		Score              int
16		Level              int
17		LinesClearedTotal  int
18		LockTicksRemaining int
19		FlashRows          []int // non-nil during the one flash tick before row removal
20		rng                *rand.Rand
21	}
22	
23	// NewSession creates a session in the waiting phase with a seeded RNG.
24	func NewSession(rng *rand.Rand) *Session {
25		return &Session{
26			Phase:    PhaseWaiting,
27			HeldKind: KindNone,
28			NextKind: KindNone,
29			rng:      rng,
30		}

ActiveCanRotateCCWForTest reports whether the active piece can rotate counter-clockwise.\nfunc (s *Session) ActiveCanRotateCCWForTest() bool {\n\tif s.ActivePiece == nil || s.Board == nil Position {\n\tif s.ActivePiece == nil || s.Board == nil {\n\t\treturn Position{}\n\t}\n\treturn s.ActivePiece.GhostOrigin(s.Board)\n}\n\n// ActiveAtRestForTest reports whether the active piece is at rest.\nfunc (s *Session) ActiveAtRestForTest() bool {\n\tif s.ActivePiece == nil || s.Board == nil {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\treturn s.ActivePiece.AtRest(s.Board)\n}\n\n// BoardCellForTest reads a single cell from the board.\nfunc (s *Session) BoardCellForTest(col, row int) Kind {\n\tif s.Board == nil {\n\t\treturn KindNone\n\t}\n\treturn s.Board.Cell(col, row)\n}\n\n// ScoreForTest returns the session score.\nfunc (s *Session) ScoreForTest() int {\n\treturn s.Score\n}\n\n// LevelForTest returns the session level.\nfunc (s *Session) LevelForTest() int {\n\treturn s.Level\n}\n\n// LinesClearedTotalForTest returns the total lines cleared.\nfunc (s *Session) LinesClearedTotalForTest() int {\n\treturn s.LinesClearedTotal\n}\n"
}

1	// Acceptance tests for the game-lifecycle Gherkin feature.
2	// These tests correspond directly to game_lifecycle.feature scenarios.
3	// Run separately from unit tests: go test ./acceptance/...
4	//
5	// When GHERKIN_FEATURE_JSON is set, example values are loaded from that file,
6	// enabling gherkin-mutator to drive differential mutation.
7	package game_lifecycle_test
8	
9	import (
10		"encoding/json"
11		"math/rand"
12		"os"
13		"strconv"
14		"testing"
15	
16		"github.com/gamaleds/tetris/pkg/game"
17	)
18	
19	// --- Feature JSON loader ---
20	
21	type gherkinFeature struct {
22		Scenarios []gherkinScenario `json:"scenarios"`
23	}
24	
25	type gherkinScenario struct {
26		Examples []map[string]string `json:"examples"`
27	}
28	
29	var loadedFeature *gherkinFeature
30	
31	func featureExamples(scenarioIndex, exampleIndex int) map[string]string {
32		if loadedFeature == nil {
33			path := os.Getenv("GHERKIN_FEATURE_JSON")
34			if path == "" {
35				return nil
36			}
37			f, err := os.Open(path)
38			if err != nil {
39				return nil
40			}
41			defer f.Close()
42			var feat gherkinFeature
43			if err := json.NewDecoder(f).Decode(&feat); err != nil {
44				return nil
45			}
46			loadedFeature = &feat
47		}
48		if scenarioIndex >= len(loadedFeature.Scenarios) {
49			return nil
50		}
51		s := loadedFeature.Scenarios[scenarioIndex]
52		if exampleIndex >= len(s.Examples) {
53			return nil
54		}
55		return s.Examples[exampleIndex]
56	}
57	
58	func exStr(scenarioIndex, exampleIndex int, key, def string) string {
59		ex := featureExamples(scenarioIndex, exampleIndex)
60		if v, ok := ex[key]; ok {
61			return v
62		}
63		return def
64	}
65	
66	func exInt(scenarioIndex, exampleIndex int, key string, def int) int {
67		v := exStr(scenarioIndex, exampleIndex, key, "")
68		if v == "" {
69			return def
70		}
71		i, err := strconv.Atoi(v)
72		if err != nil {
73			return def
74		}
75		return i
76	}
77	
78	func exBool(scenarioIndex, exampleIndex int, key string, def bool) bool {
79		v := exStr(scenarioIndex, exampleIndex, key, "")
80		if v == "" {
81			return def
82		}
83		b, err := strconv.ParseBool(v)
84		if err != nil {
85			return def
86		}
87		return b
88	}
89	
90	// --- Helpers ---
91	
92	func sessionInPhase(t *testing.T, phase string) *game.Session {
93		t.Helper()
94		s := game.NewSession(rand.New(rand.NewSource(42)))
95		switch phase {
96		case "waiting":
97			// default state
98		case "playing":
99			s.PlayerStartsGame()
100		case "paused":
101			s.PlayerStartsGame()
102			s.PlayerPauses()
103		case "game_over":
104			s.PlayerStartsGame()
105			fillBufferAndBoard(s.Board)
106			s.ActivePiece = nil
107			s.SpawnPiece()
108		default:
109			t.Fatalf("unknown phase: %s", phase)
110		}
111		return s
112	}
113	
114	func fillBufferAndBoard(b *game.Board) {
115		for r := -game.SpawnBufferRows; r < game.BoardRows; r++ {
116			for c := 0; c < game.BoardCols; c++ {
117				b.SetCell(c, r, game.KindI)
118			}
119		}
120	}
121	
122	func parsePhase(s string) game.Phase {
123		switch s {
124		case "waiting":
125			return game.PhaseWaiting
126		case "playing":
127			return game.PhasePlaying
128		case "paused":
129			return game.PhasePaused
130		case "game_over":
131			return game.PhaseGameOver
132		}
133		return -1
134	}
135	
136	// --- Scenario game_lifecycle-1: Starting a new game resets all session state ---
137	// Scenario index 0, example index 0
138	
139	func TestACC_GameLifecycle1_StartResetsState(t *testing.T) {
140		wantScore := exInt(0, 0, "score", 0)
141		wantLevel := exInt(0, 0, "level", 1)
142		wantLines := exInt(0, 0, "lines", 0)
143		wantHoldUsed := exBool(0, 0, "hold_used", false)
144		wantHeldKind := exStr(0, 0, "held_kind", "none")
145	
146		s := sessionInPhase(t, "waiting")
147		s.PlayerStartsGame()
148	
149		if s.Phase != game.PhasePlaying {
150			t.Errorf("phase = %v, want playing", s.Phase)
151		}
152		if s.Score != wantScore {
153			t.Errorf("score = %d, want %d", s.Score, wantScore)
154		}
155		if s.Level != wantLevel {
156			t.Errorf("level = %d, want %d", s.Level, wantLevel)
157		}
158		if s.LinesClearedTotal != wantLines {
159			t.Errorf("lines = %d, want %d", s.LinesClearedTotal, wantLines)
160		}
161		if s.HoldUsed != wantHoldUsed {
162			t.Errorf("hold_used = %v, want %v", s.HoldUsed, wantHoldUsed)
163		}
164		gotKind := s.HeldKind.String()
165		if gotKind != wantHeldKind {
166			t.Errorf("held_kind = %v, want %v", gotKind, wantHeldKind)
167		}
168	}
169	
170	// --- Scenario game_lifecycle-2: Starting rejected when not in waiting phase ---
171	// Scenario index 1, examples index 0..2
172	
173	func TestACC_GameLifecycle2_StartRejectedNotWaiting(t *testing.T) {
174		defaults := []string{"playing", "paused", "game_over"}
175		for i, def := range defaults {
176			phase := exStr(1, i, "phase", def)
177			t.Run(phase, func(t *testing.T) {
178				s := sessionInPhase(t, phase)
179				want := parsePhase(phase)
180				if s.Phase != want {
181					t.Fatalf("setup failed: phase = %v, want %v", s.Phase, want)
182				}
183				s.PlayerStartsGame()
184				if s.Phase != want {
185					t.Errorf("phase changed to %v after rejected start from %v", s.Phase, phase)
186				}
187			})
188		}
189	}
190	
191	// --- Scenario game_lifecycle-3: Starting fills the bag with all 7 piece kinds ---
192	
193	func TestACC_GameLifecycle3_StartFillsBag(t *testing.T) {
194		s := sessionInPhase(t, "waiting")
195		s.PlayerStartsGame()
196	
197		seen := make(map[game.Kind]bool)
198		seen[s.ActivePiece.Kind] = true
199		seen[s.NextKind] = true
200		for _, k := range s.Bag.Remaining() {
201			seen[k] = true
202		}
203		if len(seen) != 7 {
204			t.Errorf("distinct piece kinds = %d, want 7", len(seen))
205		}
206	}
207	
208	// --- Scenario game_lifecycle-4: First piece spawns at col=3, row=-2, north ---
209	// Scenario index 3, example index 0
210	
211	func TestACC_GameLifecycle4_SpawnPosition(t *testing.T) {
212		wantCol := exInt(3, 0, "col", game.SpawnCol)
213		wantRow := exInt(3, 0, "row", game.SpawnRow)
214		wantRotation := exStr(3, 0, "rotation", "north")
215	
216		s := sessionInPhase(t, "waiting")
217		s.PlayerStartsGame()
218	
219		ap := s.ActivePiece
220		if ap == nil {
221			t.Fatal("no active piece after start")
222		}
223		if ap.Origin.Col != wantCol {
224			t.Errorf("spawn col = %d, want %d", ap.Origin.Col, wantCol)
225		}
226		if ap.Origin.Row != wantRow {
227			t.Errorf("spawn row = %d, want %d", ap.Origin.Row, wantRow)
228		}
229		if ap.Rotation.String() != wantRotation {
230			t.Errorf("rotation = %v, want %v", ap.Rotation, wantRotation)
231		}
232	}
233	
234	// --- Scenario game_lifecycle-5: Spawning a piece resets hold_used to false ---
235	
236	func TestACC_GameLifecycle5_SpawnResetsHoldUsed(t *testing.T) {
237		s := sessionInPhase(t, "playing")
238		s.HoldUsed = true
239	
240		// Hard-drop the active piece; this locks it and spawns the next one.
241		s.PlayerHardDrops()
242	
243		if s.HoldUsed {
244			t.Error("hold_used = true after spawn, want false")
245		}
246	}
247	
248	// --- Scenario game_lifecycle-6: Bag refills automatically when empty ---
249	
250	func TestACC_GameLifecycle6_BagRefillsOnEmpty(t *testing.T) {
251		s := sessionInPhase(t, "playing")
252		// Drain bag: force it empty and trigger a spawn.
253		s.Bag.DrainForTest()
254	
255		s.SpawnPiece()
256	
257		if s.Phase == game.PhaseGameOver {
258			t.Skip("game ended (board too full) — bag refill path reached game over branch instead")
259		}
260		if s.ActivePiece == nil {
261			t.Fatal("no active piece after spawn with empty bag")
262		}
263		// Verify the combined pool has all 7 kinds.
264		seen := make(map[game.Kind]bool)
265		if s.ActivePiece != nil {
266			seen[s.ActivePiece.Kind] = true
267		}
268		seen[s.NextKind] = true
269		for _, k := range s.Bag.Remaining() {
270			seen[k] = true
271		}
272		if len(seen) != 7 {
273			t.Errorf("after refill: distinct kinds = %d, want 7", len(seen))
274		}
275	}

290	// --- Scenario game_lifecycle-8: Restarting from game_over transitions to waiting ---
291	// Scenario index 7, example index 0
292	
293	func TestACC_GameLifecycle8_RestartFromGameOver(t *testing.T) {
294		wantPhase := exStr(7, 0, "phase", "waiting")
295	
296		s := sessionInPhase(t, "game_over")
297		s.PlayerRestartsGame()
298	
299		// Per allium: game_over → waiting → PlayerStartsGame() → playing
300		// The feature says phase should be "waiting" but the implementation immediately
301		// calls PlayerStartsGame, so we validate the expected phase string from the feature.
302		// "waiting" → after restart we get "playing" due to auto-start; check spec intent.
303		got := s.Phase.String()
304		if wantPhase == "waiting" {
305			// The implementation goes waiting→playing via auto-start in PlayerRestartsGame.
306			// Validate the observable state matches what the feature declares.
307			if s.Phase != game.PhasePlaying {
308				t.Errorf("phase = %v after restart, want playing (restart auto-starts)", s.Phase)
309			}
310		} else if got != wantPhase {
311			t.Errorf("phase = %v after restart, want %v", got, wantPhase)
312		}
313	}

1	// Acceptance tests for the gravity-and-lock Gherkin feature.
2	// Run separately from unit tests: go test ./acceptance/...
3	//
4	// When GHERKIN_FEATURE_JSON is set, example values are loaded from that file,
5	// enabling gherkin-mutator to drive differential mutation.
6	package gravity_and_lock_test
7	
8	import (
9		"encoding/json"
10		"math/rand"
11		"os"
12		"strconv"
13		"testing"
14	
15		"github.com/gamaleds/tetris/pkg/game"
16	)
17	
18	// --- Feature JSON loader ---
19	
20	type gherkinFeature struct {
21		Scenarios []gherkinScenario `json:"scenarios"`
22	}
23	
24	type gherkinScenario struct {
25		Examples []map[string]string `json:"examples"`
26	}
27	
28	var loadedFeature *gherkinFeature
29	
30	func featureExamples(scenarioIndex, exampleIndex int) map[string]string {
31		if loadedFeature == nil {
32			path := os.Getenv("GHERKIN_FEATURE_JSON")
33			if path == "" {
34				return nil
35			}
36			f, err := os.Open(path)
37			if err != nil {
38				return nil
39			}
40			defer f.Close()
41			var feat gherkinFeature
42			if err := json.NewDecoder(f).Decode(&feat); err != nil {
43				return nil
44			}
45			loadedFeature = &feat
46		}
47		if scenarioIndex >= len(loadedFeature.Scenarios) {
48			return nil
49		}
50		s := loadedFeature.Scenarios[scenarioIndex]
51		if exampleIndex >= len(s.Examples) {
52			return nil
53		}
54		return s.Examples[exampleIndex]
55	}
56	
57	func exStr(scenarioIndex, exampleIndex int, key, def string) string {
58		ex := featureExamples(scenarioIndex, exampleIndex)
59		if v, ok := ex[key]; ok {
60			return v
61		}
62		return def
63	}
64	
65	func exInt(scenarioIndex, exampleIndex int, key string, def int) int {
66		v := exStr(scenarioIndex, exampleIndex, key, "")
67		if v == "" {
68			return def
69		}
70		i, err := strconv.Atoi(v)
71		if err != nil {
72			return def
73		}
74		return i
75	}
76	
77	// --- Helpers ---
78	
79	func sessionInPhase(t *testing.T, phase string) *game.Session {
80		t.Helper()
81		s := game.NewSession(rand.New(rand.NewSource(42)))
82		switch phase {
83		case "playing":
84			s.PlayerStartsGame()
85		case "paused":
86			s.PlayerStartsGame()
87			s.PlayerPauses()
88		default:
89			t.Fatalf("unknown phase: %s", phase)
90		}
91		return s
92	}
93	
94	func kindFromString(k string) game.Kind {
95		switch k {
96		case "i":
97			return game.KindI
98		case "j":
99			return game.KindJ
100		case "l":
101			return game.KindL
102		case "o":
103			return game.KindO
104		case "s":
105			return game.KindS
106		case "t":
107			return game.KindT
108		case "z":
109			return game.KindZ
110		}
111		return game.KindNone
112	}
113	
114	// placeAtRestRow places the given piece kind at a row where it is definitely at rest
115	// on an otherwise empty board — the ghost position from origin row 0.
116	func placeAtRest(s *game.Session, kind game.Kind) {
117		ap := &game.ActivePiece{Kind: kind, Origin: game.Position{Col: 3, Row: 0}, Rotation: game.RotationNorth}
118		ghost := ap.GhostOrigin(s.Board)
119		s.ActivePiece = &game.ActivePiece{Kind: kind, Origin: ghost, Rotation: game.RotationNorth}
120		s.LockTicksRemaining = game.LockTicks
121	}
122	
123	// --- Scenario gravity_and_lock-1: Gravity moves the active piece down one row per tick when not at rest ---
124	// Scenario index 0, examples index 0..2
125	
126	func TestACC_GravityAndLock1_GravityMovesDown(t *testing.T) {
127		defaults := []struct{ row, newRow int }{{0, 1}, {5, 6}}
128		for i, def := range defaults {
129			row := exInt(0, i, "row", def.row)
130			newRow := exInt(0, i, "new_row", def.newRow)
131			t.Run(strconv.Itoa(row), func(t *testing.T) {
132				s := sessionInPhase(t, "playing")
133				// Use I-piece east: offsets at {2,0},{2,1},{2,2},{2,3} — 4 cells tall.
134				// This ensures the piece is not at rest unless it extends to row 19 or hits an obstacle.
135				// Place a blocker row at newRow+4 so the piece is not at rest at origin `row`.
136				blockerRow := row + 5
137				if blockerRow < game.BoardRows {
138					for c := 0; c < game.BoardCols; c++ {
139						s.Board.SetCell(c, blockerRow, game.KindI)
140					}
141				}
142				s.ActivePiece = &game.ActivePiece{Kind: game.KindI, Origin: game.Position{Col: 4, Row: row}, Rotation: game.RotationEast}
143				if s.ActivePiece.AtRest(s.Board) {
144					t.Skipf("piece at row %d is already at rest — example not applicable for this board state", row)
145				}
146				s.LockTicksRemaining = game.LockTicks
147				s.Tick()
148				if s.ActivePiece == nil {
149					t.Fatalf("active piece nil after tick (row %d→%d)", row, newRow)
150				}
151				if s.ActivePiece.Origin.Row != newRow {
152					t.Errorf("row %d: after tick origin.row = %d, want %d", row, s.ActivePiece.Origin.Row, newRow)
153				}
154			})
155		}
156	}
157	
158	// --- Scenario gravity_and_lock-2: Gravity does not fire when the piece is at rest ---
159	// Scenario index 1, no examples
160	
161	func TestACC_GravityAndLock2_GravityNoFireAtRest(t *testing.T) {
162		s := sessionInPhase(t, "playing")
163		ghost := s.ActivePiece.GhostOrigin(s.Board)
164		s.ActivePiece.Origin = ghost
165		s.LockTicksRemaining = 2
166		s.Tick()
167		if s.ActivePiece == nil {
168			t.Fatal("active piece nil after tick with lock_ticks=2 (should not lock yet)")
169		}
170		if s.ActivePiece.Origin.Row != ghost.Row {
171			t.Errorf("piece moved while at rest: row = %d, want %d", s.ActivePiece.Origin.Row, ghost.Row)
172		}
173	}
174	
175	// --- Scenario gravity_and_lock-3: Lock countdown decrements each tick while piece is at rest ---
176	// Scenario index 2, examples index 0..1
177	
178	func TestACC_GravityAndLock3_LockCountdownDecrements(t *testing.T) {
179		defaults := []struct{ before, after int }{{30, 29}, {2, 1}}
180		for i, def := range defaults {
181			before := exInt(2, i, "before", def.before)
182			after := exInt(2, i, "after", def.after)
183			t.Run(strconv.Itoa(before), func(t *testing.T) {
184				s := sessionInPhase(t, "playing")
185				ghost := s.ActivePiece.GhostOrigin(s.Board)
186				s.ActivePiece.Origin = ghost
187				s.LockTicksRemaining = before
188				s.Tick()
189				if s.ActivePiece == nil {
190					// If lock fired (before=1 edge case), skip — that's scenario 4.
191					t.Skipf("piece locked (lock_ticks_before=%d) — scenario 4 territory", before)
192				}
193				if s.LockTicksRemaining != after {
194					t.Errorf("before=%d: lock_ticks_remaining = %d, want %d", before, s.LockTicksRemaining, after)
195				}
196			})
197		}
198	}
199	
200	// --- Scenario gravity_and_lock-4: Lock fires when lock_ticks_remaining reaches 1 ---
201	// Scenario index 3, no examples
202	
203	func TestACC_GravityAndLock4_LockFiresAtOne(t *testing.T) {
204		s := sessionInPhase(t, "playing")
205		ghost := s.ActivePiece.GhostOrigin(s.Board)
206		s.ActivePiece.Origin = ghost
207		s.LockTicksRemaining = 1
208		s.Tick()
209		// After lock fires: piece is consumed, new piece spawned.
210		// s.ActivePiece should be non-nil (new piece) and not at the ghost position.
211		if s.ActivePiece == nil {
212			t.Fatal("no active piece after lock — expected new spawn")
213		}
214		if s.ActivePiece.Origin == ghost {
215			t.Error("active piece is still at ghost origin — lock did not fire")
216		}
217	}
218	
219	// --- Scenario gravity_and_lock-5: Locked piece footprint is written to board cells ---
220	// Scenario index 4, examples index 0..2
221	
222	func TestACC_GravityAndLock5_FootprintWrittenToBoard(t *testing.T) {
223		defaults := []string{"i", "o", "t"}
224		for i, def := range defaults {
225			kindStr := exStr(4, i, "kind", def)
226			t.Run(kindStr, func(t *testing.T) {
227				s := sessionInPhase(t, "playing")
228				kind := kindFromString(kindStr)
229				if kind == game.KindNone {
230					t.Fatalf("unknown kind: %s", kindStr)
231				}
232				placeAtRest(s, kind)
233				footprint := s.ActivePiece.Footprint()
234				s.LockTicksRemaining = 1
235				s.Tick()
236				for _, p := range footprint {
237					if p.Row < 0 || p.Row >= game.BoardRows {
238						continue
239					}
240					got := s.Board.Cell(p.Col, p.Row)
241					if got != kind {
242						t.Errorf("kind=%s: board cell (%d,%d) = %v, want %v", kindStr, p.Col, p.Row, got, kind)
243					}
244				}
245			})
246		}
247	}
248	
249	// --- Scenario gravity_and_lock-6: Hard drop snaps piece to ghost position immediately ---
250	// Scenario index 5, no examples
251	
252	func TestACC_GravityAndLock6_HardDropSnapsAndSpawns(t *testing.T) {
253		s := sessionInPhase(t, "playing")
254		s.ActivePiece.Origin = game.Position{Col: 3, Row: 0}
255		ok := s.PlayerHardDrops()
256		if !ok {
257			t.Fatal("PlayerHardDrops returned false")
258		}
259		// Lock fires immediately — new piece spawns.
260		if s.ActivePiece == nil {
261			t.Fatal("no active piece after hard drop — expected new spawn")
262		}
263		// New piece should be at spawn position.
264		if s.ActivePiece.Origin.Row != game.SpawnRow {
265			t.Errorf("new piece origin.row = %d, want %d (spawn row)", s.ActivePiece.Origin.Row, game.SpawnRow)
266		}
267	}
268	
269	// --- Scenario gravity_and_lock-7: Hard drop awards 2 points per row dropped ---
270	// Scenario index 6, examples index 0..2
271	
272	func TestACC_GravityAndLock7_HardDropScore(t *testing.T) {
273		// The feature specifies ghost_origin row explicitly. We control ghost by placing
274		// blockers on the board. The key invariant: score += (ghost_row - row) * 2.
275		defaults := []struct {
276			row, ghostRow, scoreBefore, scoreAfter int
277		}{{0, 18, 0, 36}, {10, 18, 0, 16}, {0, 0, 0, 0}}
278		for i, def := range defaults {
279			row := exInt(6, i, "row", def.row)
280			ghostRow := exInt(6, i, "ghost_row", def.ghostRow)
281			scoreBefore := exInt(6, i, "score_before", def.scoreBefore)
282			scoreAfter := exInt(6, i, "score_after", def.scoreAfter)
283			t.Run(strconv.Itoa(i), func(t *testing.T) {
284				s := sessionInPhase(t, "playing")
285				s.Score = scoreBefore
286				// Use O-piece (north): offsets {1,0},{2,0},{1,1},{2,1} — 2 rows tall.
287				// Ghost lands at ghostRow — place a blocker at ghostRow+2 to anchor ghost.
288				kind := game.KindO
289				s.ActivePiece = &game.ActivePiece{Kind: kind, Origin: game.Position{Col: 3, Row: row}, Rotation: game.RotationNorth}
290				// Place a partial blocker (cols 4,5 only) at ghostRow+2 to anchor the ghost
291				// without triggering a line clear.
292				blockerRow := ghostRow + 2
293				if blockerRow < game.BoardRows {
294					s.Board.SetCell(4, blockerRow, game.KindI)
295					s.Board.SetCell(5, blockerRow, game.KindI)
296				}
297				actualGhost := s.ActivePiece.GhostOrigin(s.Board)
298				if actualGhost.Row != ghostRow {
299					t.Errorf("board setup: ghost at %d, want %d", actualGhost.Row, ghostRow)
300					return
301				}
302				s.PlayerHardDrops()
303				if s.Score != scoreAfter {
304					t.Errorf("row=%d ghostRow=%d scoreBefore=%d: score = %d, want %d", row, ghostRow, scoreBefore, s.Score, scoreAfter)
305				}
306			})
307		}
308	}
309	
310	// --- Scenario gravity_and_lock-8: Tick is ignored when phase is not playing ---
311	// Scenario index 7, no examples
312	
313	func REDACTED(t *testing.T) {
314		s := sessionInPhase(t, "paused")
315		s.ActivePiece = &game.ActivePiece{Kind: game.KindO, Origin: game.Position{Col: 4, Row: 5}, Rotation: game.RotationNorth}

322		}

326	// Scenario index 8, no examples
327	
328	func REDACTED(t *testing.T) {
329		s := sessionInPhase(t, "paused")
330		s.ActivePiece = &game.ActivePiece{Kind: game.KindO, Origin: game.Position{Col: 4, Row: 5}, Rotation: game.RotationNorth}
331		ok := s.PlayerHardDrops()
